And somehow it gets spoiled every weekend, over and over. After all these months, I’m supposed to believe it’s just bad luck? No. No, I can see it plain as day.\n\nSo yes, it’s the most important thing right now because he’s trying to take over my place and push me out of my own life, really. And then when I say something, everyone turns it around like I’m the problem. That’s why I’m sitting here talking to you. Not because I’m confused about what’s happening, but because nobody will take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these things happening in your house?\n\nPatient: It’s been about nine months now. Around nine months, give or take, but I remember when it started because it lines up with when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, I didn’t have this kind of problem. I knew where my things were, the house felt like my house, and I wasn’t having to watch everything so closely. Then once he came in, little by little, things started happening. At first I tried to tell myself maybe it was nothing, maybe I was just annoyed having someone else in the house, but after a while you can’t keep making excuses for it.\n\nSo yes… about nine months. That’s how long I’ve been noticing it. Since he moved in.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your wife's relative might not be intentionally trying to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because everybody keeps asking me to consider it. My wife says it, other people say it, now you’re asking it. And I tried, especially at the beginning. I really did. I told myself maybe I was being too sensitive, maybe it was just hard having him in the house, maybe things were getting moved because there were more people around.\n\nBut after a while, that explanation just doesn’t hold up. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. I’ve worked with tools my whole adult life, and I’m not the kind of man who just leaves things scattered and then blames somebody else. And it isn’t just once or twice. It’s over and over, always in a way that gets under my skin but gives him room to act innocent.\n\nAnd the fishing plans too—those don’t get spoiled by accident every weekend. Because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making something out of nothing. But I know he came into my home to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’m seeing. He came into my house to get at me, and plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen outside of your house, such as things you see on TV or read in the newspaper, are related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the TV or the newspaper or anything like that. I’m not sitting there watching the news thinking it’s about him or me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThis is in my house. That’s the part people keep trying to make fuzzy, but it isn’t fuzzy to me. But the one doing it, the one behind it in my house, is him.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your wife's relative trying to get at you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: At first, I guess I tried to talk myself out of it a little. I mean, I’m not saying the very first day I thought, “That’s it, he’s after me.” I tried to be fair about it. I thought maybe I’d put a tool somewhere else, or maybe with another person in the house things just get moved around. I gave it some time.\n\nBut no, it hasn’t really wavered, not after seeing it keep happening. The more it went on, the clearer it got. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. That’s not normal. That’s somebody doing it on purpose, and he’s the one there.\n\nSo if you mean have I ever seriously thought, “Maybe I imagined the whole thing,” no. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s been going on in my own house. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came in to get at me and try to take over my place, push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative is something that might be open to different interpretations, or is it an absolute certainty to you?\n\nPatient: At this point, it’s not really open to interpretation for me. I know people don’t like me saying that, and everybody keeps trying to soften it like maybe I’m stressed, maybe I’m seeing it wrong, maybe it’s just misunderstandings around the house. But I’ve lived in that house. I know how I keep my things. I know where I put my tools, and they don’t just move themselves.\n\nAnd it isn’t one little thing, Doctor. If it was one time, maybe I could say, all right, maybe I misplaced something. But after months of it? The same kind of thing over and over? My fishing plans getting spoiled right when I’m looking forward to them, things being shifted, the feeling that he’s edging his way into my space like it’s his place now. You can only have so many “accidents” before it stops being an accident.\n\nSo no, I don’t see it as just one interpretation among many. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. My wife doesn’t want to hear it, and if I say too much, then I’m the problem somehow.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it’s not unrealistic at all. I live there. I know how things were before he came in, and I know how they’ve been since. My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve worked with tools most of my life, I know where I set something down, I know my own habits. And my fishing plans don’t just get ruined by accident every weekend, not over and over like that. One time, sure, maybe. Two times, maybe bad luck. But after months of it, you start seeing what’s really going on.\n\nSo yes, other people can sit there and say, “That sounds unreasonable,” because they’re not the ones dealing with it day after day. They’re not the ones being pushed out of their own place, little by little. To me it’s plain as day. He came into that house to get at me, and he’s been trying to take over my place. That’s not me being unrealistic. That’s me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear outside of your house, like on the street or from neighbors, are somehow related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the street or the neighbors, not really. I’m not walking around thinking every car going by or every neighbor talking is part of it. That’s not what I’ve been trying to say.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is what’s happening in my own house, with him there. That’s where it is. My tools being moved, things not being where I left them, my fishing plans getting ruined over and over like it’s timed to get under my skin. That’s not some neighbor down the road doing that, and it’s not something I heard outside and twisted around. It’s in the house. It started with him being there, and after all these months it’s plain to me he’s been trying to push me out and make the place feel like it’s his.\n\nSo if you mean do I think the whole neighborhood is involved, no. But do I think what’s happening around my home is connected to him getting at me? Yes, absolutely. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’ve seen in my own place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about your wife's relative trying to get at you, or is that something you cannot imagine being wrong about?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to ask myself that, because everybody keeps pushing me to. My wife looks at me like I’m being unfair, and other people act like I’m making something out of nothing, so yes, I’ve sat there and thought, “All right, what if I’m wrong?” But then I go back over it in my head, and it just doesn’t add up any other way.\n\nIf it was one tool missing, or one plan getting messed up, I could admit maybe I forgot where I put something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. I’ve misplaced things before like anybody else. But this is not that. I know my own garage, I know where I leave my tools, I know the way I set things up. They don’t just move themselves. And my fishing getting spoiled over and over, right when I’m looking forward to getting out of the house for some peace, that doesn’t feel like coincidence after months of it.\n\nSo can I imagine being wrong? Honestly, not really. Not with all of it together. That’s why I’m here, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little. Everyone else can call it whatever they want, but I’m the one living with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else in your household believes that your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. And somehow it gets spoiled every weekend, over and over. After all these months, I’m supposed to believe it’s just bad luck? No. No, I can see it plain as day.\n\nSo yes, it’s the most important thing right now because he’s trying to take over my place and push me out of my own life, really. And then when I say something, everyone turns it around like I’m the problem. That’s why I’m sitting here talking to you. Not because I’m confused about what’s happening, but because nobody will take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these things happening in your house?\n\nPatient: It’s been about nine months now. Around nine months, give or take, but I remember when it started because it lines up with when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, I didn’t have this kind of problem. I knew where my things were, the house felt like my house, and I wasn’t having to watch everything so closely. Then once he came in, little by little, things started happening. At first I tried to tell myself maybe it was nothing, maybe I was just annoyed having someone else in the house, but after a while you can’t keep making excuses for it.\n\nSo yes… about nine months. That’s how long I’ve been noticing it. Since he moved in.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your wife's relative might not be intentionally trying to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because everybody keeps asking me to consider it. My wife says it, other people say it, now you’re asking it. And I tried, especially at the beginning. I really did. I told myself maybe I was being too sensitive, maybe it was just hard having him in the house, maybe things were getting moved because there were more people around.\n\nBut after a while, that explanation just doesn’t hold up. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. I’ve worked with tools my whole adult life, and I’m not the kind of man who just leaves things scattered and then blames somebody else. And it isn’t just once or twice. It’s over and over, always in a way that gets under my skin but gives him room to act innocent.\n\nAnd the fishing plans too—those don’t get spoiled by accident every weekend. That was my time, my peace, the one thing I had to look forward to. Then somehow, again and again, something happens to interfere with it. After all these months, I’m supposed to just say, “Oh, maybe he didn’t mean it”? No. I can see plain as day what’s going on.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, really. Because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making something out of nothing. But I know he came into my home to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. And it’s not just one wrench misplaced one time. It’s the way things keep happening, the pattern of it, always just enough to throw me off and make me look like I’m overreacting.\n\nAnd the fishing plans—same thing. Every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident over and over. That was my time, my peace, and he knows it.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’m seeing. He came into my house to get at me, and plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen outside of your house, such as things you see on TV or read in the newspaper, are related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the TV or the newspaper or anything like that. I’m not sitting there watching the news thinking it’s about him or me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThis is in my house. That’s the part people keep trying to make fuzzy, but it isn’t fuzzy to me. It’s not some message from outside or something I read somewhere. It’s what I see happening right in front of me, day after day. My tools are where I put them, and then they’re not. My fishing plans get interfered with again and again, always right when I’m trying to have that bit of peace for myself.\n\nAnd I know everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making connections that aren’t there, but I’m telling you, this is not about the television. It’s about him being in my home and getting at me in these little ways, then standing back like he didn’t do anything. After all these months, I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is the only person involved in trying to get at you, or could there be others?\n\nPatient: I think it’s him. I mean, he’s the one in the house, he’s the one who came in and started all this. I’m not saying there’s some big group or anything like that, if that’s what you’re asking. I don’t have proof of other people doing things. What I know is what’s been happening right under my own roof.\n\nEverybody keeps trying to make it sound like I’m imagining it, but I know my tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every weekend. After months of that, you start to see the pattern whether other people want to admit it or not.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, it’s him. He’s the one trying to get at me. He’s the one trying to take over my place and push me out. Could my wife be making excuses for him, or not wanting to see it? Sure, maybe. But the one doing it, the one behind it in my house, is him.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your wife's relative trying to get at you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: At first, I guess I tried to talk myself out of it a little. I mean, I’m not saying the very first day I thought, “That’s it, he’s after me.” I tried to be fair about it. I thought maybe I’d put a tool somewhere else, or maybe with another person in the house things just get moved around. I gave it some time.\n\nBut no, it hasn’t really wavered, not after seeing it keep happening. The more it went on, the clearer it got. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. That’s not normal. That’s somebody doing it on purpose, and he’s the one there.\n\nSo if you mean have I ever seriously thought, “Maybe I imagined the whole thing,” no. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s been going on in my own house. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came in to get at me and try to take over my place, push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative is something that might be open to different interpretations, or is it an absolute certainty to you?\n\nPatient: At this point, it’s not really open to interpretation for me. I know people don’t like me saying that, and everybody keeps trying to soften it like maybe I’m stressed, maybe I’m seeing it wrong, maybe it’s just misunderstandings around the house. But I’ve lived in that house. I know how I keep my things. I know where I put my tools, and they don’t just move themselves.\n\nAnd it isn’t one little thing, Doctor. If it was one time, maybe I could say, all right, maybe I misplaced something. But after months of it? The same kind of thing over and over? My fishing plans getting spoiled right when I’m looking forward to them, things being shifted, the feeling that he’s edging his way into my space like it’s his place now. You can only have so many “accidents” before it stops being an accident.\n\nSo no, I don’t see it as just one interpretation among many. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. My wife doesn’t want to hear it, and if I say too much, then I’m the problem somehow.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it’s not unrealistic at all. I live there. I know how things were before he came in, and I know how they’ve been since. My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve worked with tools most of my life, I know where I set something down, I know my own habits. And my fishing plans don’t just get ruined by accident every weekend, not over and over like that. One time, sure, maybe. Two times, maybe bad luck. But after months of it, you start seeing what’s really going on.\n\nSo yes, other people can sit there and say, “That sounds unreasonable,” because they’re not the ones dealing with it day after day. They’re not the ones being pushed out of their own place, little by little. To me it’s plain as day. He came into that house to get at me, and he’s been trying to take over my place. That’s not me being unrealistic. That’s me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear outside of your house, like on the street or from neighbors, are somehow related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the street or the neighbors, not really. I’m not walking around thinking every car going by or every neighbor talking is part of it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I mean, it isn’t just one thing, that’s what people don’t seem to understand. If it was one thing, maybe I could let it go. But it’s the pattern.

My tools are the biggest thing. I’ve worked with my hands my whole life, and I know where I put things. I don’t just toss tools anywhere. I have certain places for them, and when I come back and something’s moved, or turned around, or not where I left it, I notice. People act like, “Oh, maybe you misplaced it,” but no. My tools don’t move themselves. And this started after he came into the house.

Then there’s my fishing. That’s my time to get out, clear my head, just have a little peace. And somehow, over and over, my plans get spoiled. Not once, not twice—weekend after weekend. Something happens, something gets in the way, and it always feels timed, like he knows that’s when I’m trying to have something for myself.

And after months of that, you start to see it plain as day. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place, little by little, and push me out of my own home. That’s the evidence to me—the tools, the fishing, the way it keeps happening. It’s not an accident every time.

I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. That’s not me being unrealistic. That’s me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear outside of your house, like on the street or from neighbors, are somehow related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the street or the neighbors, not really. I’m not walking around thinking every car going by or every neighbor talking is part of it. That’s not what I’ve been trying to say.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is what’s happening in my own house, with him there. That’s where it is. That’s not some neighbor down the road doing that, and it’s not something I heard outside and twisted around. It’s in the house. It started with him being there, and after all these months it’s plain to me he’s been trying to push me out and make the place feel like it’s his.\n\nSo if you mean do I think the whole neighborhood is involved, no. But do I think what’s happening around my home is connected to him getting at me? Yes, absolutely. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’ve seen in my own place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about your wife's relative trying to get at you, or is that something you cannot imagine being wrong about?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to ask myself that, because everybody keeps pushing me to. My wife looks at me like I’m being unfair, and other people act like I’m making something out of nothing, so yes, I’ve sat there and thought, “All right, what if I’m wrong?” But then I go back over it in my head, and it just doesn’t add up any other way.\n\nIf it was one tool missing, or one plan getting messed up, I could admit maybe I forgot where I put something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. I’ve misplaced things before like anybody else. But this is not that. I know my own garage, I know where I leave my tools, I know the way I set things up. They don’t just move themselves. Honestly, not really. Not with all of it together. That’s why I’m here, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little. Everyone else can call it whatever they want, but I’m the one living with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else in your household believes that your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. My wife sure doesn’t, or at least she won’t say it. She acts like I’m just worked up, like I’m seeing things the wrong way, and that’s part of what makes this so hard. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve got some problem with him for no reason.\n\nBut I know what’s going on in that house. I know how things were before he came in, and I know what started happening after. My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had my tools a certain way for years. I can tell when something’s been touched or shifted or put back wrong. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend, not over and over like that. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came into that house to get at me.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think anyone else in the household is willing to admit it. They either don’t see it, or they don’t want to see it. But I’m the one being pushed out of my own place, little by little. He’s trying to take over, and everyone keeps making it sound like I’m the one causing trouble just because I’m saying it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific incident or evidence that led you to believe your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, it isn’t just one thing, that’s what people don’t seem to understand. If it was one thing, maybe I could let it go. But it’s the pattern.\n\nMy tools are the biggest thing. After all these months, I’m supposed to just say, “Oh, maybe he didn’t mean it”? No. I can see plain as day what’s going on.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, really. Because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making something out of nothing. But I know he came into my home to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. And it’s not just one wrench misplaced one time. It’s the way things keep happening, the pattern of it, always just enough to throw me off and make me look like I’m overreacting.\n\nAnd the fishing plans—same thing. Every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident over and over. That was my time, my peace, and he knows it.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’m seeing. He came into my house to get at me, and plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen outside of your house, such as things you see on TV or read in the newspaper, are related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the TV or the newspaper or anything like that. I’m not sitting there watching the news thinking it’s about him or me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThis is in my house. That’s the part people keep trying to make fuzzy, but it isn’t fuzzy to me. It’s not some message from outside or something I read somewhere. It’s what I see happening right in front of me, day after day. My tools are where I put them, and then they’re not. My fishing plans get interfered with again and again, always right when I’m trying to have that bit of peace for myself.\n\nAnd I know everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making connections that aren’t there, but I’m telling you, this is not about the television. It’s about him being in my home and getting at me in these little ways, then standing back like he didn’t do anything. After all these months, I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is the only person involved in trying to get at you, or could there be others?\n\nPatient: I think it’s him. I mean, he’s the one in the house, he’s the one who came in and started all this. I’m not saying there’s some big group or anything like that, if that’s what you’re asking. I don’t have proof of other people doing things. What I know is what’s been happening right under my own roof.\n\nEverybody keeps trying to make it sound like I’m imagining it, but I know my tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every weekend. After months of that, you start to see the pattern whether other people want to admit it or not.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, it’s him. He’s the one trying to get at me. He’s the one trying to take over my place and push me out. Could my wife be making excuses for him, or not wanting to see it? Sure, maybe. But the one doing it, the one behind it in my house, is him.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your wife's relative trying to get at you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: At first, I guess I tried to talk myself out of it a little. I mean, I’m not saying the very first day I thought, “That’s it, he’s after me.” I tried to be fair about it. I thought maybe I’d put a tool somewhere else, or maybe with another person in the house things just get moved around. I gave it some time.\n\nBut no, it hasn’t really wavered, not after seeing it keep happening. The more it went on, the clearer it got. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. That’s not normal. That’s somebody doing it on purpose, and he’s the one there.\n\nSo if you mean have I ever seriously thought, “Maybe I imagined the whole thing,” no. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s been going on in my own house. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came in to get at me and try to take over my place, push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative is something that might be open to different interpretations, or is it an absolute certainty to you?\n\nPatient: At this point, it’s not really open to interpretation for me. I know people don’t like me saying that, and everybody keeps trying to soften it like maybe I’m stressed, maybe I’m seeing it wrong, maybe it’s just misunderstandings around the house. But I’ve lived in that house. I know how I keep my things. I know where I put my tools, and they don’t just move themselves.\n\nAnd it isn’t one little thing, Doctor. If it was one time, maybe I could say, all right, maybe I misplaced something. But after months of it? The same kind of thing over and over? My fishing plans getting spoiled right when I’m looking forward to them, things being shifted, the feeling that he’s edging his way into my space like it’s his place now. You can only have so many “accidents” before it stops being an accident.\n\nSo no, I don’t see it as just one interpretation among many. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. My wife doesn’t want to hear it, and if I say too much, then I’m the problem somehow.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it’s not unrealistic at all. I live there. I know how things were before he came in, and I know how they’ve been since. My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve worked with tools most of my life, I know where I set something down, I know my own habits. And my fishing plans don’t just get ruined by accident every weekend, not over and over like that. One time, sure, maybe. Two times, maybe bad luck. But after months of it, you start seeing what’s really going on.\n\nSo yes, other people can sit there and say, “That sounds unreasonable,” because they’re not the ones dealing with it day after day. They’re not the ones being pushed out of their own place, little by little. To me it’s plain as day. He came into that house to get at me, and he’s been trying to take over my place. That’s not me being unrealistic. That’s me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear outside of your house, like on the street or from neighbors, are somehow related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the street or the neighbors, not really. I’m not walking around thinking every car going by or every neighbor talking is part of it. That’s not what I’ve been trying to say.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is what’s happening in my own house, with him there. That’s where it is. My tools being moved, things not being where I left them, my fishing plans getting ruined over and over like it’s timed to get under my skin. That’s not some neighbor down the road doing that, and it’s not something I heard outside and twisted around. It’s in the house. It started with him being there, and after all these months it’s plain to me he’s been trying to push me out and make the place feel like it’s his.\n\nSo if you mean do I think the whole neighborhood is involved, no. But do I think what’s happening around my home is connected to him getting at me? Yes, absolutely. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’ve seen in my own place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about your wife's relative trying to get at you, or is that something you cannot imagine being wrong about?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to ask myself that, because everybody keeps pushing me to. My wife looks at me like I’m being unfair, and other people act like I’m making something out of nothing, so yes, I’ve sat there and thought, “All right, what if I’m wrong?” But then I go back over it in my head, and it just doesn’t add up any other way.\n\nIf it was one tool missing, or one plan getting messed up, I could admit maybe I forgot where I put something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. I’ve misplaced things before like anybody else. But this is not that. I know my own garage, I know where I leave my tools, I know the way I set things up. They don’t just move themselves. And my fishing getting spoiled over and over, right when I’m looking forward to getting out of the house for some peace, that doesn’t feel like coincidence after months of it.\n\nSo can I imagine being wrong? Honestly, not really. Not with all of it together. That’s why I’m here, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little. Everyone else can call it whatever they want, but I’m the one living with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else in your household believes that your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. My wife sure doesn’t, or at least she won’t say it. She acts like I’m just worked up, like I’m seeing things the wrong way, and that’s part of what makes this so hard. He’s trying to take over, and everyone keeps making it sound like I’m the one causing trouble just because I’m saying it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific incident or evidence that led you to believe your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Before he moved in, no, I didn’t have that thought. I mean, there wasn’t any reason to. This all started when my wife’s relative came into our home, and it’s been going on about nine months now.

I keep my things a certain way, always have. I work with my hands, I know where I put things, and then suddenly they’re not where they should be. And my fishing plans—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled or interfered with, and everybody wants me to believe it’s just bad luck or coincidence. But after months of this, you start seeing the pattern clear as day.\n\nHe’s trying to take over my place. That’s what it feels like—no, that’s what it is. He’s pushing in, pushing me out, and then everybody looks at me like I’m the problem for noticing. So I came here because maybe if I say it plainly to someone outside the family, someone will actually listen instead of acting like I don’t know what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this relative's actions are targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Absolutely. I don’t see how it could be anything else. This isn’t just some general family tension or him being careless in the house. It’s aimed at me.\n\nI’m the one whose tools end up moved around. I’m the one who goes to get something where I know I left it, because I keep my things in order, and suddenly it’s not there. And then I’m supposed to believe I misplaced it? No. I’ve been working with my hands for years. I know my tools.\n\nAnd the fishing—same thing. Those are my plans, my weekends, the one thing I try to do for myself, and somehow they keep getting spoiled. Once or twice, maybe you call that coincidence. But every weekend, after months of it? No, that’s a pattern. He knows what matters to me, and he keeps interfering with it.\n\nSo yes, it’s targeted. He came into my house to get at me, to push me out, to make it feel like it isn’t my place anymore. And then everyone acts like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. I can see what he’s doing plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything that other people cannot?\n\nPatient: Seen or heard things other people can’t? I mean… not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I’m not hearing voices talking to me or seeing people standing there who aren’t there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is real things in the house. Tools moved, plans interfered with, things not being where they should be. Other people may not be paying attention, or they don’t want to admit what’s happening, but that doesn’t mean I’m seeing something that isn’t there. It’s more like they’re choosing not to see the pattern.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like… visions or sounds nobody else can hear. I just know what I’ve been noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the relative's actions are part of a larger plan or conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan… yes, I mean, I don’t know what else you’d call it when it keeps happening over and over and it all points in the same direction.\n\nI’m not saying there’s some whole town full of people involved or anything like that, but in that house, yes, it feels planned. It’s not random. They just don’t. I know where I keep my things.\n\nAnd the fishing, that’s not an accident every weekend. One time, sure. Twice, maybe. But month after month? No. He knows what matters to me, and he interferes with it. It’s like he’s trying to wear me down, make me look unreasonable, make it seem like I don’t belong in my own place anymore.\n\nSo yes, I believe there’s a plan behind it. Maybe he doesn’t write it down or say it out loud, but I can see it plain as day. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. But I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your relative's actions against you are the most important thing happening in your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… how could it not be? It’s happening in my own house. That’s the thing people don’t seem to understand. This isn’t some little disagreement or something I can just leave at work and come home from. It’s where I live. It’s my tools, my weekends, my space, my peace of mind.\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s going on. My wife’s relative came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves. They’re not just wandering off the bench or ending up somewhere else by accident. I know where I put them. I’ve worked with tools for years, I’m not careless with them.\n\nAnd the fishing plans—same thing. That was the one thing I had, you know? Something to look forward to, something that was mine. I knew where my things were, the house felt like my house, and I wasn’t having to watch everything so closely. Then once he came in, little by little, things started happening. That’s how long I’ve been noticing it. My wife says it, other people say it, now you’re asking it. And I tried, especially at the beginning. I really did. I told myself maybe I was being too sensitive, maybe it was just hard having him in the house, maybe things were getting moved because there were more people around.\n\nBut after a while, that explanation just doesn’t hold up. And it isn’t just once or twice. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. And it’s not just one wrench misplaced one time. It’s the way things keep happening, the pattern of it, always just enough to throw me off and make me look like I’m overreacting.\n\nAnd the fishing plans—same thing. Every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident over and over. That was my time, my peace, and he knows it.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’m seeing. He came into my house to get at me, and plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen outside of your house, such as things you see on TV or read in the newspaper, are related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the TV or the newspaper or anything like that. I’m not sitting there watching the news thinking it’s about him or me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThis is in my house. That’s the part people keep trying to make fuzzy, but it isn’t fuzzy to me. It’s not some message from outside or something I read somewhere. It’s what I see happening right in front of me, day after day. My tools are where I put them, and then they’re not. My fishing plans get interfered with again and again, always right when I’m trying to have that bit of peace for myself.\n\nAnd I know everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making connections that aren’t there, but I’m telling you, this is not about the television. It’s about him being in my home and getting at me in these little ways, then standing back like he didn’t do anything. After all these months, I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is the only person involved in trying to get at you, or could there be others?\n\nPatient: I think it’s him. I mean, he’s the one in the house, he’s the one who came in and started all this. I’m not saying there’s some big group or anything like that, if that’s what you’re asking. I don’t have proof of other people doing things. What I know is what’s been happening right under my own roof.\n\nEverybody keeps trying to make it sound like I’m imagining it, but I know my tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every weekend. After months of that, you start to see the pattern whether other people want to admit it or not.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, it’s him. He’s the one trying to get at me. He’s the one trying to take over my place and push me out. Could my wife be making excuses for him, or not wanting to see it? Sure, maybe. But the one doing it, the one behind it in my house, is him.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your wife's relative trying to get at you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: At first, I guess I tried to talk myself out of it a little. I mean, I’m not saying the very first day I thought, “That’s it, he’s after me.” I tried to be fair about it. I thought maybe I’d put a tool somewhere else, or maybe with another person in the house things just get moved around. I gave it some time.\n\nBut no, it hasn’t really wavered, not after seeing it keep happening. The more it went on, the clearer it got. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. That’s not normal. That’s somebody doing it on purpose, and he’s the one there.\n\nSo if you mean have I ever seriously thought, “Maybe I imagined the whole thing,” no. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s been going on in my own house. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came in to get at me and try to take over my place, push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative is something that might be open to different interpretations, or is it an absolute certainty to you?\n\nPatient: At this point, it’s not really open to interpretation for me. I know people don’t like me saying that, and everybody keeps trying to soften it like maybe I’m stressed, maybe I’m seeing it wrong, maybe it’s just misunderstandings around the house. But I’ve lived in that house. I know how I keep my things. I know where I put my tools, and they don’t just move themselves.\n\nAnd it isn’t one little thing, Doctor. If it was one time, maybe I could say, all right, maybe I misplaced something. But after months of it? The same kind of thing over and over? My fishing plans getting spoiled right when I’m looking forward to them, things being shifted, the feeling that he’s edging his way into my space like it’s his place now. You can only have so many “accidents” before it stops being an accident.\n\nSo no, I don’t see it as just one interpretation among many. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. My wife doesn’t want to hear it, and if I say too much, then I’m the problem somehow.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it’s not unrealistic at all. I live there. I know how things were before he came in, and I know how they’ve been since. My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve worked with tools most of my life, I know where I set something down, I know my own habits. And my fishing plans don’t just get ruined by accident every weekend, not over and over like that. One time, sure, maybe. Two times, maybe bad luck. But after months of it, you start seeing what’s really going on.\n\nSo yes, other people can sit there and say, “That sounds unreasonable,” because they’re not the ones dealing with it day after day. They’re not the ones being pushed out of their own place, little by little. To me it’s plain as day. He came into that house to get at me, and he’s been trying to take over my place. That’s not me being unrealistic. That’s me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear outside of your house, like on the street or from neighbors, are somehow related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the street or the neighbors, not really. I’m not walking around thinking every car going by or every neighbor talking is part of it. That’s not what I’ve been trying to say.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is what’s happening in my own house, with him there. That’s where it is. My tools being moved, things not being where I left them, my fishing plans getting ruined over and over like it’s timed to get under my skin. That’s not some neighbor down the road doing that, and it’s not something I heard outside and twisted around. It’s in the house. It started with him being there, and after all these months it’s plain to me he’s been trying to push me out and make the place feel like it’s his.\n\nSo if you mean do I think the whole neighborhood is involved, no. But do I think what’s happening around my home is connected to him getting at me? Yes, absolutely. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’ve seen in my own place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be wrong about your wife's relative trying to get at you, or is that something you cannot imagine being wrong about?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to ask myself that, because everybody keeps pushing me to. My wife looks at me like I’m being unfair, and other people act like I’m making something out of nothing, so yes, I’ve sat there and thought, “All right, what if I’m wrong?” But then I go back over it in my head, and it just doesn’t add up any other way.\n\nIf it was one tool missing, or one plan getting messed up, I could admit maybe I forgot where I put something. I’m not saying I’m perfect. I’ve misplaced things before like anybody else. But this is not that. I know my own garage, I know where I leave my tools, I know the way I set things up. They don’t just move themselves. And my fishing getting spoiled over and over, right when I’m looking forward to getting out of the house for some peace, that doesn’t feel like coincidence after months of it.\n\nSo can I imagine being wrong? Honestly, not really. Not with all of it together. That’s why I’m here, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little. Everyone else can call it whatever they want, but I’m the one living with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else in your household believes that your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. My wife sure doesn’t, or at least she won’t say it. She acts like I’m just worked up, like I’m seeing things the wrong way, and that’s part of what makes this so hard. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve got some problem with him for no reason.\n\nBut I know what’s going on in that house. He’s trying to take over, and everyone keeps making it sound like I’m the one causing trouble just because I’m saying it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific incident or evidence that led you to believe your wife's relative is trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it is based on facts, yes. That’s what frustrates me so much, because everybody talks like it’s just something in my head, but these are things that actually happen in the house.

I mean, if someone paid attention, they could verify it. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve had my way of keeping them for years, and when something is shifted or put back wrong, I can tell. Another person could look and see they’re not where they were supposed to be, if they cared enough to notice.

And the fishing plans—same thing. It’s not like I’m saying I had one bad weekend and made up a story. It’s weekend after weekend, something gets spoiled, something gets in the way. After all these months, that’s not just bad luck. To me it’s plain as day he came into this house to get at me, and he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.

So yes, I think it could be verified, but the problem is nobody wants to admit what they’re seeing. They act like I’m imagining it because that’s easier than saying he’s doing it.

And my fishing plans—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled or interfered with, and everybody wants me to believe it’s just bad luck or coincidence. But after months of this, you start seeing the pattern clear as day.\n\nHe’s trying to take over my place. That’s what it feels like—no, that’s what it is. He’s pushing in, pushing me out, and then everybody looks at me like I’m the problem for noticing. So I came here because maybe if I say it plainly to someone outside the family, someone will actually listen instead of acting like I don’t know what’s going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this relative's actions are targeted specifically at you?\n\nPatient: Yes. Absolutely. I don’t see how it could be anything else. This isn’t just some general family tension or him being careless in the house. It’s aimed at me.\n\nI’m the one whose tools end up moved around. I’m the one who goes to get something where I know I left it, because I keep my things in order, and suddenly it’s not there. And then I’m supposed to believe I misplaced it? No. I’ve been working with my hands for years. I know my tools.\n\nAnd the fishing—same thing. Those are my plans, my weekends, the one thing I try to do for myself, and somehow they keep getting spoiled. Once or twice, maybe you call that coincidence. But every weekend, after months of it? No, that’s a pattern. He knows what matters to me, and he keeps interfering with it.\n\nSo yes, it’s targeted. He came into my house to get at me, to push me out, to make it feel like it isn’t my place anymore. And then everyone acts like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. I can see what he’s doing plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Have you seen or heard anything that other people cannot?\n\nPatient: Seen or heard things other people can’t? I mean… not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I’m not hearing voices talking to me or seeing people standing there who aren’t there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is real things in the house. Tools moved, plans interfered with, things not being where they should be. Other people may not be paying attention, or they don’t want to admit what’s happening, but that doesn’t mean I’m seeing something that isn’t there. It’s more like they’re choosing not to see the pattern.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like… visions or sounds nobody else can hear. I just know what I’ve been noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the relative's actions are part of a larger plan or conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan… yes, I mean, I don’t know what else you’d call it when it keeps happening over and over and it all points in the same direction.\n\nI’m not saying there’s some whole town full of people involved or anything like that, but in that house, yes, it feels planned. It’s not random. My wife’s relative came into my home and started getting at me in these little ways, the kind of ways where if I complain, everyone can say, “Oh, maybe you just misplaced it,” or “Maybe the fishing plans just didn’t work out.” But tools don’t move themselves. They just don’t. I know where I keep my things.\n\nAnd the fishing, that’s not an accident every weekend. One time, sure. Twice, maybe. But month after month? No. He knows what matters to me, and he interferes with it. It’s like he’s trying to wear me down, make me look unreasonable, make it seem like I don’t belong in my own place anymore.\n\nSo yes, I believe there’s a plan behind it. Maybe he doesn’t write it down or say it out loud, but I can see it plain as day. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. But I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your relative's actions against you are the most important thing happening in your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… how could it not be? It’s happening in my own house. That’s the thing people don’t seem to understand. This isn’t some little disagreement or something I can just leave at work and come home from. It’s where I live. It’s my tools, my weekends, my space, my peace of mind.\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s going on. And then when I say something, everyone turns it around like I’m the problem. That’s why I’m sitting here talking to you. Not because I’m confused about what’s happening, but because nobody will take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these things happening in your house?\n\nPatient: It’s been about nine months now. Around nine months, give or take, but I remember when it started because it lines up with when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, I didn’t have this kind of problem. I knew where my things were, the house felt like my house, and I wasn’t having to watch everything so closely. Then once he came in, little by little, things started happening. That’s how long I’ve been noticing it. Since he moved in.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your wife's relative might not be intentionally trying to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because everybody keeps asking me to consider it. My wife says it, other people say it, now you’re asking it. And I tried, especially at the beginning. I really did. I told myself maybe I was being too sensitive, maybe it was just hard having him in the house, maybe things were getting moved because there were more people around.\n\nBut after a while, that explanation just doesn’t hold up. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. No. No, I can see it plain as day.\n\nSo yes, it’s the most important thing right now because he’s trying to take over my place and push me out of my own life, really. And then when I say something, everyone turns it around like I’m the problem. That’s why I’m sitting here talking to you. Not because I’m confused about what’s happening, but because nobody will take it seriously.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these things happening in your house?\n\nPatient: It’s been about nine months now. Around nine months, give or take, but I remember when it started because it lines up with when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, I didn’t have this kind of problem. I knew where my things were, the house felt like my house, and I wasn’t having to watch everything so closely. Then once he came in, little by little, things started happening. At first I tried to tell myself maybe it was nothing, maybe I was just annoyed having someone else in the house, but after a while you can’t keep making excuses for it.\n\nSo yes… about nine months. That’s how long I’ve been noticing it. Since he moved in.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your wife's relative might not be intentionally trying to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it because everybody keeps asking me to consider it. My wife says it, other people say it, now you’re asking it. And I tried, especially at the beginning. I really did. I told myself maybe I was being too sensitive, maybe it was just hard having him in the house, maybe things were getting moved because there were more people around.\n\nBut after a while, that explanation just doesn’t hold up. My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I put them. I’ve worked with tools my whole adult life, and I’m not the kind of man who just leaves things scattered and then blames somebody else. And it isn’t just once or twice. It’s over and over, always in a way that gets under my skin but gives him room to act innocent.\n\nAnd the fishing plans too—those don’t get spoiled by accident every weekend. That was my time, my peace, the one thing I had to look forward to. Then somehow, again and again, something happens to interfere with it. After all these months, I’m supposed to just say, “Oh, maybe he didn’t mean it”? No. I can see plain as day what’s going on.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, really. Because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making something out of nothing. But I know he came into my home to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and people keep wanting me to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative trying to get at you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know you probably want me to say there’s a chance, but if I’m being honest, no. Not after all this time.\n\nAt the beginning, maybe. I could see asking that then. When he first came into the house, I tried to give it room. I thought, well, maybe I’m just not used to another person being there, maybe things are getting shifted around, maybe I’m noticing more because I’m irritated. I’m not unreasonable. I did think about that.\n\nBut now? After months of it? My tools don’t move themselves. I know where I leave them. I’ve been working with tools for years, and I’m not careless with them. And it’s not just one wrench misplaced one time. It’s the way things keep happening, the pattern of it, always just enough to throw me off and make me look like I’m overreacting.\n\nAnd the fishing plans—same thing. Every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident over and over. That was my time, my peace, and he knows it.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what I’m seeing. He came into my house to get at me, and plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that events or things that happen outside of your house, such as things you see on TV or read in the newspaper, are related to your wife's relative's actions against you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it’s coming from the TV or the newspaper or anything like that. I’m not sitting there watching the news thinking it’s about him or me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThis is in my house. That’s the part people keep trying to make fuzzy, but it isn’t fuzzy to me. It’s not some message from outside or something I read somewhere. It’s what I see happening right in front of me, day after day. My tools are where I put them, and then they’re not. My fishing plans get interfered with again and again, always right when I’m trying to have that bit of peace for myself.\n\nAnd I know everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’m making connections that aren’t there, but I’m telling you, this is not about the television. After all these months, I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is the only person involved in trying to get at you, or could there be others?\n\nPatient: I think it’s him. I mean, he’s the one in the house, he’s the one who came in and started all this. I’m not saying there’s some big group or anything like that, if that’s what you’re asking. I don’t have proof of other people doing things. And my fishing plans don’t just get spoiled by accident every weekend. After months of that, you start to see the pattern whether other people want to admit it or not.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, it’s him. He’s the one trying to get at me. He’s the one trying to take over my place and push me out. Could my wife be making excuses for him, or not wanting to see it? Sure, maybe. But the one doing it, the one behind it in my house, is him.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about your wife's relative trying to get at you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: At first, I guess I tried to talk myself out of it a little. I mean, I’m not saying the very first day I thought, “That’s it, he’s after me.” I tried to be fair about it. I thought maybe I’d put a tool somewhere else, or maybe with another person in the house things just get moved around. That’s somebody doing it on purpose, and he’s the one there.\n\nSo if you mean have I ever seriously thought, “Maybe I imagined the whole thing,” no. After all these months, it’s plain as day to me that he came in to get at me and try to take over my place, push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative is something that might be open to different interpretations, or is it an absolute certainty to you?\n\nPatient: At this point, it’s not really open to interpretation for me. I know people don’t like me saying that, and everybody keeps trying to soften it like maybe I’m stressed, maybe I’m seeing it wrong, maybe it’s just misunderstandings around the house. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out little by little.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your wife's relative trying to get at you could be considered unreasonable or unrealistic by others?\n\nPatient: I’m sure other people would call it unreasonable. That’s kind of why I’m sitting here, isn’t it? Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I’ve made this whole thing up in my head or I’m overreacting. My wife doesn’t want to hear it, and if I say too much, then I’m the problem somehow.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it’s not unrealistic at all. I live there. That’s not me being unrealistic.
